拉丁美洲和加勒比地区关于登革热 疫苗 的知识、态度和做法:范围界定审查 拉丁美洲和加勒比地区预防登革热的预防措施、行动和实践:美洲预防登革热的修订、态度和实践拉丁与加勒比: uma revisão de escopo

Knowledge, attitudes and practices about dengue vaccines in Latin America and the Caribbean: a scoping review Conocimiento, actitudes y prácticas sobre las vacunas contra el dengue en América Latina y el Caribe: una revisión de alcance Conhecimento, atitudes e práticas sobre vacinas contra a dengue na América Latina e no Caribe: uma revisão de escopo
作者： Arredondo-Nontol M, Garcia-Villegas D, Gonzaga-Vilchez R, Izquierdo-Marchan J, Ramos-Salina R, Castromonte-Izquierdo C, Arredondo-Nontol R, Reto N
摘要中文翻译本研究旨在综合该地区与登革热 疫苗接种 相关的 KAP 的现有证据。根据 JBI 指南进行了范围界定审查。研究选择和数据提取是独立进行的并且一式两份。在波多黎各进行的一项研究的证据表明,COVID-19 大流行与接受登革热 疫苗 的意愿增加有关。对实践的研究较少,结果显示 疫苗接种 计划的完成率较低,表明态度和吸收之间存在差距。加强教育策略并促进医疗保健专业人员和民众之间的有效沟通——特别是在引入新的 疫苗 期间和在不良事件的背景下——可以支持明智的决策并提高登革热 疫苗 的吸收率。疫苗;健康知识、态度、实践方法本次范围界定审查遵循 JBI 建立的方法框架 19 以及 Arksey & O’Malley 20 的基本原理。审查过程包括五个关键步骤:确定研究问题;确定相关研究;选择研究;绘制数据图表;并整理、总结和报告结果。摘要登革热仍然是拉丁美洲和加勒比地区的一个主要公共卫生问题,疫苗接种 已成为减少严重疾病的关键战略。然而,其成功实施取决于民众的知识、态度和实践(KAP)。检索在国际和地区数据库中进行,包括 MEDLINE/PubMed、Scopus、Embase、Web of Science、SciELO 和 Virtual Health Library。原始研究被纳入,没有语言或日期限制。包括十六项研究。大多数研究报告称,关于...的知识有限。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本次范围界定审查旨在综合拉丁美洲和加勒比地区与登革热 疫苗接种 相关的知识、态度和实践 (KAP) 的现有证据。该综述的重点是 登革病毒 1-4 和登革热疾病,重点是了解公众对登革热 疫苗 的看法和行为。
证据基础与方法
审查遵循 PRISMA-ScR 检查清单。检索在国际和地区数据库中进行,包括 MEDLINE/PubMed、Scopus、Embase、Web of Science、SciELO 和 Virtual Health Library。人口包括拉丁美洲和加勒比地区的所有个人,没有性别、年龄、教育水平或职业的限制。中心概念是关于登革热 疫苗接种 的 KAP 评估。该综述包括 16 项研究,其中 4 项采用定性设计,10 项采用定量设计,2 项采用横断面设计的混合方法。
共识与结论
大多数研究报告称,对登革热 疫苗 的了解有限,特别是在安全性、有效性和监管批准方面。对实践的研究较少,但研究结果表明 疫苗接种 计划的完成率较低,这表明态度与实际采用之间存在差距。医疗保健专业人员对 Dengvaxia 更加熟悉,但其使用、禁忌症以及与其他 疫苗 或合并症的相互作用仍然存在不确定性。观察到对 疫苗接种 的积极态度,通常以感知的安全性和有效性为条件,教育水平越高,态度越有利。
争议与缺口
研究中存在异质性,一些研究报告 疫苗 认知度较低,另一些研究则指出积极态度。对不良事件的担忧和不愿参加临床试验的情况持续存在。证据差距包括参与者对 疫苗 特征、风险和资格标准的了解有限,以及在实际项目中不完全遵守 疫苗接种 时间表。审查还强调了对 疫苗 可用性的认识参差不齐,一些群体(例如家长和学校工作人员)表现出熟悉程度有限。
科研与实践启示
未来的研究应侧重于提高公众对登革热 疫苗 的认识,特别是家长和学校人员的认识。需要对 疫苗 的安全性、有效性和资格标准进行更好的教育。此外,现实世界的计划应解决对 疫苗接种 时间表的遵守问题,并探索提高 疫苗 吸收率的策略。需要进一步的研究来了解 Dengvaxia 与合并症和其他 疫苗 的相互作用及其给药细节。

Knowledge, attitudes and practices about dengue vaccines in Latin America and the Caribbean: a scoping review Conocimiento, actitudes y prácticas sobre las vacunas contra el dengue en América Latina y el Caribe: una revisión de alcance Conhecimento, atitudes e práticas sobre vacinas contra a dengue na América Latina e no Caribe: uma revisão de escopo

Authors: Arredondo-Nontol M, Garcia-Villegas D, Gonzaga-Vilchez R, Izquierdo-Marchan J, Ramos-Salina R, Castromonte-Izquierdo C, Arredondo-Nontol R, Reto N
Original abstract (English)
This study aimed to synthesize available evidence on KAP related to dengue vaccination in the region. A scoping review was conducted following JBI guidelines. Study selection and data extraction were conducted independently and in duplicate. Evidence from a study conducted in Puerto Rico indicated that the COVID-19 pandemic was associated with increased intention to receive the dengue vaccine. Practices were less studied, and findings showed low completion of vaccination schedules, indicating a gap between attitudes and uptake. Strengthening educational strategies and promoting qualified communication between healthcare professionals and the population - particularly during the introduction of new vaccines and in the context of adverse events - may support informed decision-making and improve dengue vaccine uptake.Keywords: Dengue; Vaccines; Health Knowledge, Attitudes, Practice MethodsThis scoping review followed the methodological framework established by the JBI 19 and the rationale of Arksey & O’Malley 20 . The review process included five key steps: identifying the research question; identifying relevant studies; selecting studies; charting data; and collating, summarizing, and reporting results. AbstractDengue remains a major public health concern in Latin America and the Caribbean, where vaccination has emerged as a key strategy to reduce severe disease. However, its successful implementation depends on population knowledge, attitudes, and practices (KAP). Searches were performed in international and regional databases, including MEDLINE/PubMed, Scopus, Embase, Web of Science, SciELO, and the Virtual Health Library. Original studies were included without language or date restrictions. Sixteen studies were included. Most studies reported limited knowledge regardi...
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This scoping review aimed to synthesize available evidence on knowledge, attitudes, and practices (KAP) related to dengue vaccination in Latin America and the Caribbean. The review focused on dengue viruses 1–4 and dengue disease, with an emphasis on understanding public perception and behavior toward dengue vaccines.
Evidence base and method
The review followed the PRISMA-ScR checklist. Searches were conducted in international and regional databases, including MEDLINE/PubMed, Scopus, Embase, Web of Science, SciELO, and the Virtual Health Library. The population included all individuals in Latin America and the Caribbean, with no restrictions on sex, age, education level, or occupation. The central concept was the evaluation of KAP regarding dengue vaccination. The review included 16 studies, with 4 using qualitative designs, 10 quantitative designs, and 2 mixed-methods with cross-sectional designs.
Consensus and conclusions
Most studies reported limited knowledge about dengue vaccines, particularly regarding safety, efficacy, and regulatory approval. Practices were less studied, but findings indicated low completion of vaccination schedules, suggesting a gap between attitudes and actual uptake. Healthcare professionals showed greater familiarity with Dengvaxia, but uncertainties remained about its use, contraindications, and interactions with other vaccines or comorbidities. Positive attitudes toward vaccination were observed, often conditional on perceived safety and efficacy, with higher educational levels associated with more favorable attitudes.
Controversies and gaps
There was heterogeneity in the studies, with some reporting low vaccine awareness and others noting positive attitudes. Concerns about adverse events and reluctance to participate in clinical trials persisted. Evidence gaps included limited understanding of vaccine characteristics, risks, and eligibility criteria among participants, as well as incomplete adherence to vaccination schedules in real-world programs. The review also highlighted uneven awareness of vaccine availability, with some groups (e.g., parents and school personnel) showing limited familiarity.
Research and practice implications
Future research should focus on improving public awareness of dengue vaccines, particularly among parents and school personnel. There is a need for better education on vaccine safety, efficacy, and eligibility criteria. Additionally, real-world programs should address adherence to vaccination schedules and explore strategies to enhance vaccine uptake. Further studies are required to understand the interactions of Dengvaxia with comorbidities and other vaccines, as well as its administration details.

DENV-NS1 drives novel microRNAs to disrupt cerebral endothelial barrier functions.

A 高优先级公共卫生新闻

美洲卫生组织呼吁加强流行病学监测和应对能力,应对美洲地区登革热和寨卡病毒病例增加

PAHO calls for strengthened surveillance and preparedness amid increases in dengue and chikungunya in the Americas
新闻简报The Pan American Health Organization (PAHO) has called for enhanced surveillance and preparedness in the Americas amid rising dengue and chikungunya cases. As of mid-September 2026, 1.69 million suspected dengue cases were reported, including 3,413 severe cases and 571 deaths, with increases observed in the Andean, Central American, and Caribbean subregions. Chikungunya transmission has also surged, with 213,919 suspected cases and 88 deaths in 20 countries. PAHO attributes these trends to the anticipated intensification of the El Niño phenomenon and the peak transmission season in South America. The organization recommends strengthening epidemiological, virological, and entomological surveillance, increasing diagnostic capacity, and preparing health services to manage potential case surges. Integrated mosquito control measures, including eliminating breeding sites and community engagement, are emphasized to mitigate the spread of these diseases.
查看新闻五要素
时间
PAHO于2026年10月20日发布了流行病学更新,事件日期为2026年9月中旬。加强流行病学监测的呼吁正值南美洲即将进入寨卡病毒季节高峰,预计由于厄尔尼诺现象的加剧而更加严重。
地点与对象
美洲地区,特别是南美洲,以及安第斯地区、中美洲和加勒比地区子区域。受影响的人群包括该地区所有国家和领土,重点是登革热和寨卡病毒病例增加的地区。
事件
PAHO呼吁加强流行病学监测和应对能力,以应对美洲地区近期登革热和寨卡病毒病例的增加。该组织指出寨卡病毒在一些多年未见的地区重新出现,并注意到厄尔尼诺现象预计的加剧以及寨卡病毒季节高峰的到来。
规模、影响与风险
As of mid-September 2026, 1.69 million suspected dengue cases were reported, including 3,413 severe cases and 571 deaths. Chikungunya transmission has increased, with 213,919 suspected cases and 88 deaths in 20 countries. The update also notes transmission in new areas, outbreaks outside historical seasons, and longer transmission periods.
应对、状态与不确定性
PAHO敦促各国加强流行病学监测,提高诊断能力,并为潜在病例增加做好卫生服务准备。该组织建议采取综合蚊虫控制措施、环境管理以及社区参与预防和控制工作。
A high priorityPublic-health news

PAHO calls for strengthened surveillance and preparedness amid increases in dengue and chikungunya in the Americas

News Brief
The Pan American Health Organization (PAHO) has called for enhanced surveillance and preparedness in the Americas amid rising dengue and chikungunya cases. As of mid-September 2026, 1.69 million suspected dengue cases were reported, including 3,413 severe cases and 571 deaths, with increases observed in the Andean, Central American, and Caribbean subregions. Chikungunya transmission has also surged, with 213,919 suspected cases and 88 deaths in 20 countries. PAHO attributes these trends to the anticipated intensification of the El Niño phenomenon and the peak transmission season in South America. The organization recommends strengthening epidemiological, virological, and entomological surveillance, increasing diagnostic capacity, and preparing health services to manage potential case surges. Integrated mosquito control measures, including eliminating breeding sites and community engagement, are emphasized to mitigate the spread of these diseases.

Search Search Home News PAHO calls for strengthened surveillance and preparedness amid increases in dengue and chikungunya in the Americas PAHO calls for strengthened surveillance and preparedness amid increases in dengue and chikungunya in the Americas Copied to clipboard The Organization highlights recent increases in dengue cases in several countries and the resurgence of chikungunya transmission in areas where the virus had not circulated for several years, as South America approaches its peak transmission season. Washington, D.C., October 21, 2026 (PAHO) – The Pan American Health Organization (PAHO) called on countries in the Americas to strengthen surveillance and prepare health services in response to recent increases in dengue observed in several countries and increased and renewed chikungunya transmission in countries and territories where viral circulation had not been documented for several years. The call comes amid the anticipated intensification of the El Niño phenomenon and as South America approaches the peak transmission season for these diseases. In an epidemiological update published on October 20, PAHO reported that as of mid-September, 1.69 million suspected dengue cases had been reported in the Region, including 3,413 severe cases and 571 deaths. Although the cumulative number of reported cases represents a 58% decrease compared with the same period in 2025, this decline has not been uniform across the Region: increases have been observed in recent weeks in countries in the Andean, Central American, and Caribbean subregions. Circulation of all four dengue virus serotypes has also been identified in the Americas in 2026. PAHO also highlighted the chikungunya situation, with transmission increasing between late 2025 and 2026 and reemerging in countries and territories that had not documented viral circulation for several years. As of mid-September, 213,919 suspected cases had been reported, of which 78,481 were confirmed, along with 88 deaths in 20 countries in the Region. The update also points to transmission in new areas, outbreaks outside historical transmission seasons, longer transmission periods, and increased reporting of more severe clinical manifestations. Strengthening prevention and preparing health services In response, PAHO urged countries to strengthen epidemiological, virological, and entomological surveillance, increase diagnostic capacity, and ensure early detection and timely clinical management of cases. The Organization also recommended stepping up integrated measures to control Aedes mosquitoes, the main vectors of these diseases. PAHO recommends that health services prepare to respond to potential increases in cases at all levels of care, with particular emphasis on strengthening primary health care capacity to recognize dengue warning signs early and prevent progression to severe disease. Hospitals should also be prepared to manage cases with dengue warning signs and severe cases of these diseases. Eliminating mosquito breeding sites remains a key measure to reduce mosquito populations. PAHO recommends strengthening environmental management, eliminating containers that collect water, and promoting the active participation of communities, families, local authorities, and other sectors in prevention and control efforts. Time
PAHO reported the epidemiological update on October 20, 2026, with the event date being mid-September 2026. The call for strengthened surveillance comes as South America approaches its peak transmission season, which is anticipated to intensify due to the El Niño phenomenon.
Location and population
The Americas, with specific mention of South America, the Andean, Central American, and Caribbean subregions. The affected population includes countries and territories in the Region, with a focus on areas where dengue and chikungunya transmission has increased.
Event
PAHO called for strengthened surveillance and preparedness in response to recent increases in dengue and chikungunya cases in the Americas. The organization highlighted the resurgence of chikungunya in areas where it had not circulated for several years, and noted the anticipated intensification of the El Niño phenomenon and peak transmission season.
Scale, impact and risk
As of mid-September 2026, 1.69 million suspected dengue cases were reported, including 3,413 severe cases and 571 deaths. Chikungunya transmission has increased, with 213,919 suspected cases and 88 deaths in 20 countries. The update also notes transmission in new areas, outbreaks outside historical seasons, and longer transmission periods.
Response, status and uncertainty
PAHO urged countries to strengthen surveillance, increase diagnostic capacity, and prepare health services for potential increases in cases. The organization recommended integrated mosquito control measures, environmental management, and community participation in prevention and control efforts.

A 高优先级公共卫生新闻

美洲卫生组织呼吁加强监测和应对能力,应对美洲地区登革热和寨卡病毒活动增加 - 美洲卫生组织(PAHO)

PAHO calls for strengthened surveillance and preparedness amid increases in dengue and chikungunya in the Americas - Pan American Health Organization (PAHO)
新闻简报2026年10月21日,美洲卫生组织(PAHO)发布了一项官方警报,指出美洲地区登革热和寨卡病毒活动正在上升。主任Jarbas Barbosa呼吁成员国加强监测和应对能力,以更好地检测和应对这些虫媒病毒威胁。尽管该声明确认了病例数量在上升,但并未提供具体的感染人数、死亡人数或受影响社区的具体数据。该指导重点在于加强区域早期预警系统和公共卫生应对能力,但未包含调查、检测或控制措施的细节,因此疫情的确切规模和风险水平仍不确定。该声明反映了美洲卫生组织在该地区协调卫生应对措施的作用,并强调了继续监测登革热和寨卡病毒趋势的必要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
美洲卫生组织的通知发布于2026年10月21日,同一天也报告了呼吁加强监测和应对能力的情况。
地点与对象
该声明涉及美洲地区以及成员国中登革热和寨卡病毒高风险人群。
事件
美洲卫生组织(PAHO)由主任Jarbas Barbosa代表,发布了一项公开呼吁,要求各国加强监测和应对能力,以应对登革热和寨卡病毒报告病例数量的增加。该报告是明确的观察结果,而非确认的暴发,并被作为官方卫生警报提出。
规模、影响与风险
通知中提到美洲地区登革热和寨卡病毒病例数量在增加,但并未提供具体的病例数、死亡人数或暴露数据。因此,无法从现有证据中量化疫情的确切规模和风险水平。
应对、状态与不确定性
美洲卫生组织建议各国加强监测系统和应对措施;未详细描述调查、检测或控制行动,疫情增加的规模仍存在不确定性。
A high priorityPublic-health news

PAHO calls for strengthened surveillance and preparedness amid increases in dengue and chikungunya in the Americas - Pan American Health Organization (PAHO)

News Brief
On 21 October 2026, the Pan American Health Organization (PAHO) issued an official alert highlighting rising dengue and chikungunya activity across the Americas. Director Jarbas Barbosa called on member states to boost surveillance and preparedness to better detect and respond to these arboviral threats. While the announcement confirms that case numbers are climbing, it does not provide specific counts of infections, deaths, or affected communities. The guidance focuses on strengthening regional early‐warning systems and public‐health readiness, but details of investigations, testing, or control measures are not included, leaving the precise scale and risk level uncertain. This statement reflects PAHO’s role in coordinating health responses in the region and underscores the need for continued monitoring of dengue and chikungunya trends.

The statement refers to the Americas region and to populations at risk of dengue and chikungunya across the member countries.

2026年10月20日太平洋地区疫情及新发疾病警报 - ReliefWeb

Epidemic and emerging disease alerts in the Pacific as of 20 October 2026 - ReliefWeb
新闻简报截至2026年10月20日,登革病毒 暴发及相关疾病警报在太平洋多个地区被报告。在马绍尔群岛,记录了156例阳性病例,共检测了501份样本。萨摩亚报告了64例新病例,而瓦努阿图在马勒库拉岛宣布出现登革热暴发。汤加出现了137例流感样病例,其中67例重症急性呼吸道感染(SARI)患者住院治疗。媒介控制及监测措施正在受影响地区持续进行。
查看新闻五要素
时间
跳过主要内容 帮助 登录 ReliefWeb 内容搜索 搜索内容 你在找什么?
地点与对象
马绍尔群岛、埃贝伊、马朱罗、萨摩亚、瓦努阿图、新喀里多尼亚、新西兰、汤加、密克罗尼西亚联邦。
事件
登革病毒 暴发及相关疾病警报。
规模、影响与风险
501 specimens tested, 156 positive cases (Majuro 124, Ebeye 32), 13 cumulative hospitalizations, 5.4 per 1,000 attack rate in Majuro, 3.3 per 1,000 in Ebeye, 64 new cases in Samoa, 22 dengue cases in Vanuatu, 137 influenza-like illness cases in Tonga, 156 of 336 tested ILI cases positive for influenza A, 67 SARI patients hospitalized, 3 deaths among SARI cases.
应对、状态与不确定性
马绍尔群岛、萨摩亚、瓦努阿图和汤加对登革热(DENV-1)发出红色警报,萨摩亚对登革热(DENV-1 & DENV-2)发出蓝色警报,正在进行媒介控制及监测,病例调查,媒介控制,社区意识提升,免费登革热检测,麻疹病例数量经专家评审组确认为确诊病例,流感疫情持续,呼吸道疾病仍具有显著影响,汤加对甲型流感发出红色警报。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Epidemic and emerging disease alerts in the Pacific as of 20 October 2026 - ReliefWeb

News Brief
Dengue virus outbreaks and related disease alerts were reported in several Pacific countries as of 20 October 2026. In the Marshall Islands, 156 positive cases were recorded, with 501 specimens tested. Samoa reported 64 new cases, while Vanuatu declared a dengue outbreak on Malekula Island. Tonga saw 137 influenza-like illness cases, with 67 SARI patients hospitalized. Vector control and surveillance measures are ongoing in affected areas.

Search|t Updates Countries Disasters Organizations Topics Jobs Training Informing humanitarians worldwide 24/7 — a service provided by UN OCHA World + 15 more Epidemic and emerging disease alerts in the Pacific as of 20 October 2026 Format Map Source SPC Posted 20 Oct 2026 Originally published 20 Oct 2026 Attachments Download Map (PDF | 534.32 KB | 2 pages) Highlights/updates since the last map was sent on PacNet on 13 October 2026: Dengue • Marshall Islands: The 15th Outbreak Situation Reports on dengue in Majuro and Ebeye covering 15 August to 15 October, record 501 specimens tested (Majuro 411, Ebeye 90) and 156 positive cases, confirmed and probable (Majuro 124, Ebeye 32). This is an increase of 21 cases from the 137 cases reported on 05 October (12th Outbreak Situation Reports). The positivity has fallen to 30% in Majuro and 36% in Ebeye. No deaths have been reported. Cumulative hospitalisations rose to thirteen. The attack rate is 5.4 per 1,000 in Majuro and 3.3 per 1,000 in Ebeye. Only DENV-1 has been detected. Vector control has been completed at the households of positive cases, 26 schools and 60 churches have been sprayed, and surveillance is being strengthened on the neighbouring islands. On 08 October 2026, the Cabinet declared a state of disaster for El Niño and the dengue outbreak. The red alert for dengue (DENV-1) remains in effect. – Source: Republic of the Marshall Islands Ministry of Health and Human Services, Outbreak of Dengue Fever Cases in Majuro & Ebeye, 13th, 14th and 15th Outbreak Situation Reports shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 19 October 2026. • Samoa: Dengue Fever Outbreak Situation Report Issue No. 77, covering EpiWeek 40 (28 September to 04 October 2026), reports 64 new cases, up 73% on the previous week, with one laboratory-confirmed case and three new admissions. No new intensive care admission and no death were reported. Since 01 January 2025, 20,374 clinically diagnosed cases, 5,547 laboratory-confirmed cases and nine dengue-related deaths have been recorded. DENV-1 accounts for 52% and DENV-2 for 48% of serotyped cases, and children under 15 years for 73% of cases. The blue alert for dengue (DENV-1 & DENV-2) remains in effect. – Source: Ministry of Health Samoa, Dengue Fever Outbreak Situation Report Issue No. 77 shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 16 October 2026. • Vanuatu: On 15 October 2026, the Ministry of Health declared a dengue outbreak on Malekula Island, Malampa Province, after four dengue cases were reported between 02 and 12 October in the Central North East and North Malekula Area Councils, with evidence of local transmission. Malekula is the fourth island with a declared outbreak, after Efate, Santo and Tanna. The same day, Malampa Provincial Health reported 22 dengue cases on Malekula, including six laboratory-confirmed, in North East and Central Malekula, with no deaths and no hospitalisations. Surveillance, case investigation, mosquito control and community awareness are being strengthened, and free dengue testing is offered at Norsup Hospital. The red alert for dengue (DENV-1) remains in effect for Vanuatu. – Sources: Vanuatu Ministry of Health, Dengue outbreak declared on Malekula Island, accessed on 20 October 2026 and Malampa Provincial Health, Dengue fever i stap long Malampa, accessed on 20 October 2026. Measles • Papua New Guinea: The National Department of Health weekly bulletin for EpiWeek 41 (05 to 11 October 2026) has changed the measles case count: cases are now counted as confirmed by the Expert Review Group instead of laboratory IgM-positive results. On this basis, three confirmed measles cases have been reported in 2026 (East Sepik 2, New Ireland 1), against 17 laboratory-confirmed cases reported up to EpiWeek 40. The last onset date remains 15 September 2026. The measles-rubella vaccination campaign continues to target all children under seven in every district with a detected measles or rubella case. The red alert for measles remains in effect for Papua New Guinea. – Source: Papua New Guinea National Department of Health, Public Health Events Weekly Bulletin, EpiWeek 41, shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 15 October 2026. Respiratory diseases • New Caledonia: The Department of Health and Social Affairs (DASS) influenza bulletin 2026-#10 of 16 October 2026 reports that the epidemic continues, with circulation still intense but levelling off: 130 laboratory-confirmed cases in EpiWeek 41 (05 to 11 October), against 151 in week 40. It is the first decrease since the start of the epidemic, and test positivity fell from 65% to 55%. A(H1N1)pdm09 remains largely predominant (98 of 130, the other 32 influenza A not subtyped). Hospital impact remains significant, with 67 patients hospitalised in EpiWeek 41 (median age 68 years), and intensive care bed occupancy has exceeded 80% on 8 of the last 14 days. Emergency department activity has stabilised at a high level (1,832 presentations in EpiWeek 41, against an average of 1,300), and acute respiratory infections accounted for 13% of presentations, mainly influenza. The red alert for influenza A remains in effect for New Caledonia. – Source: Direction des Affaires Sanitaires et Sociales de Nouvelle-Calédonie, Vigilance DASS bulletin 2026-#10, influenza epidemic shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 16 October 2026. • New Zealand: For the week ending 11 October 2026, the all-cause SARI hospitalisation rate in the Auckland region decreased again and remains in the low activity band. Influenza remained the most commonly detected virus among SARI patients, and SARI influenza test positivity rose from 18.6 to 25.9%, while positivity among sentinel GP influenza-like illness cases fell slightly from 17.6 to 15.8%. Pacific peoples have had the highest cumulative rates of influenza-, RSV- and SARS-CoV-2-positive SARI hospitalisation in 2026. The blue alert for influenza A remains in effect for New Zealand. – Source: PHF Science, New Zealand Acute Respiratory Illness dashboard, accessed on 20 October 2026. • Tonga: The weekly syndromic surveillance report for EpiWeek 41 (05 to 11 October 2026) records 137 influenza-like illness cases, against 99 in week 40, the seventh consecutive weekly increase and the highest weekly count of 2026, and severe acute respiratory infections rose from 14 to 20, above the alert threshold. Between 03 August and 11 October, 156 of 336 tested ILI cases (46.4%) were positive for influenza A, against 89 a week earlier, and 67 SARI patients were hospitalised. The number of deaths among SARI cases is unchanged at three, two of them laboratory-confirmed influenza A. A red alert for influenza A is issued for Tonga. – Source: Tonga Ministry of Health, Weekly Communicable Diseases Syndromic Surveillance, EpiWeek 41 shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 20 October 2026. Rotavirus • Federated States of Micronesia: The Yap weekly bulletin for EpiWeek 41 (05 to 11 October 2026) shows diarrhoea on alert for an eighth consecutive week, although cases decreased to 9 from 16 in EpiWeek 40, still above the alert threshold of 7. Of four patients tested with a gastrointestinal multiplex PCR panel, three were positive: one adenovirus and EPEC co-infection, one EAEC and one rotavirus A. Among 100 diarrhoea cases in children aged 0 to 5 years this season, 34 had completed rotavirus vaccination. A public health advisory on diarrhoeal illness remains in place. The blue alert for rotavirus remains in effect for the Federated States of Micronesia (Yap State). – Source: Yap State Department of Health Services, Communicable Disease Weekly Bulletin, EpiWeek 41 shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 14 October 2026. Other Information: Chikungunya • New Zealand: In surveillance week 41 (10 to 16 October 2026), one confirmed chikungunya case was notified in New Zealand in a person who had been to the Cook Islands during the incubation period. Year to date, four confirmed chikungunya cases (Cook Islands 1, Indonesia 2, Sri Lanka 1) and two probable cases have been notified, all with overseas exposure. – Source: PHF Science, NZ Arbovirus Notifications by Country, week 41 shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 19 October 2026. Dengue • Hawai’i: On 13 October 2026, the Hawaiʻi Department of Health reported a new travel-related dengue case on Oʻahu, in a person exposed while travelling in a region where dengue is common. It is the ninth dengue case reported in Hawaiʻi in 2026. No link to previous cases has been found, mosquito inspection and control were carried out around the affected area, and the risk of local transmission is considered low. – Source: Hawaiʻi Department of Health, DOH reports another travel-related dengue virus case, accessed on 20 October 2026. One Health - High pathogenicity avian influenza • Australia: The national H5 bird flu dashboard recorded 713 confirmed events in wildlife as of 16 October 2026, against 700 on 09 October: South Australia 331, Victoria 245, New South Wales 71, Tasmania 53, Western Australia 10, Queensland 2 and Jervis Bay Territory 1. H5 has not been reported from any Pacific Island country or territory to date. – Sources: Australian Government Department of Agriculture, Fisheries and Forestry, H5 bird flu latest data accessed on 20 October 2026. Respiratory diseases • French Polynesia: The health surveillance bulletin no. 37, covering data to EpiWeek 41 (05 to 11 October 2026), reports 10 influenza A cases in EpiWeek 41, against four in EpiWeek 40, three subtyped A(H1N1)pdm09 and three A(H3N2). Five patients were hospitalised, one of them in intensive care. Influenza waves in French Polynesia usually follow the Northern Hemisphere, between October and April. The 2025-2026 wave caused 200 hospitalisations, 23 intensive care admissions and 13 deaths. The influenza vaccination campaign will start on 26 October 2026. – Source: Agence de régulation de l'action sanitaire et sociale (ARASS), Bulletin de Surveillance Sanitaire de Polynésie française n°37 shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 20 October 2026. 佛罗里达登革热暴发重申协作防控的重要性 - PCT杂志

Florida Dengue Outbreak Reinforces Importance of Collaborative Control Efforts - PCT magazine
新闻简报截至2026年10月3日,佛罗里达州卫生部报告了在八个县共发现296例本地感染的登革热病例,强调了公共卫生官员、害虫管理专业人士和居民之间协作防控的必要性。暴发已被确认,但未明确说明风险等级或对卫生系统的影响。响应状态显示该情况正在调查中,重点在于实施防控措施。文章强调了社区和专业人员合作在管理登革热暴发中的重要性,同时也提及其他与害虫相关的行业活动和事件。这份报告提醒我们,害虫控制和公共卫生管理仍面临持续的挑战。

Florida Dengue Outbreak Reinforces Importance of Collaborative Control Efforts - PCT magazine

News Brief
The Florida Department of Health reported 296 locally acquired cases of dengue fever across eight counties as of October 3, 2026, underscoring the need for collaborative efforts among public health officials, pest management professionals, and residents to control the spread. The outbreak is confirmed, but no explicit risk level or health-system impact was stated. The response status indicates that the situation is under investigation, with a focus on implementing control measures. The article highlights the importance of community and professional cooperation in managing dengue outbreaks, while also mentioning other pest-related activities and events in the industry. This report serves as a reminder of the ongoing challenges in pest control and public health management.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

Qdenga 登革热 疫苗:为何泰米尔纳德邦的卡达亚纳卢尔试点可能成为转折点 - Oneindia

Qdenga Dengue Vaccine: Why Tamil Nadu’s Kadayanallur Pilot Could Be A Turning Point - Oneindia
新闻简报一项使用 Qdenga 疫苗 的登革热 疫苗接种 试点项目正在印度泰米尔纳德邦的卡达亚纳卢尔进行,作为探索新预防策略的一部分。据 Oneindia 报道,该项目由印度医学研究委员会(ICMR)进行评估,旨在评估 疫苗 在高 发病率 区域减少登革热病例的效果。该 疫苗 由日本制药公司 Takeda 开发,已在印度获批用于 4-60 岁人群,是一种活病毒、四价 疫苗,旨在保护所有四种登革病毒血清型(DENV-1、DENV-2、DENV-3、DENV-4)。卫生官员将监测 疫苗 接种率、不良事件以及实验室确认的登革热感染变化。这些发现可能不仅影响卡达亚纳卢尔,还可能影响印度更广泛的登革热预防策略。该试点项目包括两剂接种,第二剂在第一剂接种后三个月进行,且不是印度本土的登革热 疫苗 候选疫苗 DengiAll。该试点项目是更大努力的一部分,旨在将 疫苗接种 整合到印度针对登革热的公共卫生措施中,因为该地区由于降雨、温度和城市化等因素,登革热问题反复出现。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该试点项目于 2026 年 10 月 19 日在泰米尔纳德邦的卡达亚纳卢尔进行,第二剂计划在第一剂接种后三个月进行。活动日期未明确说明,但该项目仍在进行中。
地点与对象
该试点项目在印度泰米尔纳德邦 Tenkasi 县的卡达亚纳卢尔镇进行。该项目涉及一个经常面临登革热的群体,因为该地区登革病毒 发病率 率较高。
事件
一项使用 Qdenga 疫苗 的登革热 疫苗接种 试点项目正在印度泰米尔纳德邦的卡达亚纳卢尔进行,作为印度探索登革热预防策略的一部分。该项目由 Oneindia 报道,并由印度医学研究委员会(ICMR)进行评估。
规模、影响与风险
该试点项目旨在评估 Qdenga 疫苗 在高 发病率 区域减少登革热病例的效果。该项目未报告具体病例数、死亡人数或暴露情况,但强调了 疫苗接种 补充现有策略的潜力。风险未明确说明,但该 疫苗 设计用于保护所有四种登革病毒血清型。
应对、状态与不确定性
该项目正在进行中,卫生官员正在追踪 疫苗 接种率、不良事件以及登革热感染的变化。这些发现可能影响印度更广泛的预防策略。来源是一篇完整文章,未注明任何限制。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Qdenga Dengue Vaccine: Why Tamil Nadu’s Kadayanallur Pilot Could Be A Turning Point - Oneindia

News Brief
A dengue vaccination pilot using the Qdenga vaccine is underway in Kadayanallur, Tamil Nadu, India, as part of efforts to explore new prevention strategies. The programme, reported by Oneindia, is being reviewed by the Indian Council of Medical Research (ICMR) and aims to assess the vaccine's effectiveness in reducing dengue cases in a high-incidence area. The vaccine, developed by Japanese pharmaceutical company Takeda, is approved for ages 4-60 in India and is a live-attenuated, tetravalent vaccine designed to protect against all four dengue serotypes (DENV-1, DENV-2, DENV-3, DENV-4). Health officials will monitor vaccine uptake, adverse events, and changes in laboratory-confirmed dengue infections. The findings could have implications beyond Kadayanallur, potentially shaping broader dengue prevention strategies in India. The programme involves two doses, with the second given three months after the first, and is not India's indigenous dengue vaccine candidate, DengiAll. The pilot is part of a larger effort to integrate vaccination into public health measures against dengue, which remains a recurring issue in the region due to factors like rainfall, temperature, and urban growth.
Original source text (English)
Wed, Oct 21, 2026 Mumbai 33°C Delhi 32°C Mumbai 33°C Chennai 28°C Kolkata 32°C Bengaluru 26°C Hyderabad 32°C Dussehra Choose Language English தமிழ் हिन्दी ಕನ್ನಡ മലയാളം తెలుగు বাংলা ગુજરાતી ଓଡ଼ିଆ मराठी ਪੰਜਾਬ Get Updates Get notified of breaking news, exclusive insights, and must-see stories! News India International Entertainment Cricket Sports Business Videos Videos Photos Local News Bengaluru New Delhi Mumbai Chennai Kolkata Pune Salem Belgaum Ahmedabad Hyderabad Mangalore Elections Upcoming Elections Tamil Nadu Assembly Elections Kerala Assembly Elections West Bengal Assembly Elections Assam Assembly Elections Puducherry Assembly Elections Loksabha Elections Politicians Political Parties Commodity Price Vegetables Price Flowers Price Fruits Price Chicken Price Mutton Price Fish Price Egg Price Weather AQI Lottery Results Prayer Timings Temples in India Quotes Power Cut Dam Water Level Tamil Calendar Malayalam Calendar News India International Entertainment Cricket Sports Business Videos City Bengaluru New Delhi Mumbai Chennai Kolkata Pune Salem Belgaum Ahmedabad Hyderabad Mangalore Sports Pro Kabaddi League Cricket Football Photos Astro Services Movies Bollywood Hollywood Tamil Telugu Malayalam Kannada Television Photo Gallery Lifestyle Health Beauty Cookery Horoscope Festivals Google Doodle Productivity Auto Car News Bike News Reviews How-To Photos EMI Calculator Offbeat Explore Cars Gadgets Mobiles Reviews Photo Gallery Latest Mobiles Upcoming Mobiles Phone Finder Money Quiz Home / Chennai Qdenga Dengue Vaccine: Why Tamil Nadu’s Kadayanallur Pilot Could Be A Turning Point Chennai oi-Prakash KL By Oneindia Desk Published: Monday, October 19, 2026, 12:14 [IST] Add as a preferred source on Google For years, the fight against dengue in India has largely followed the same playbook - control mosquitoes, prevent breeding, identify cases early and treat patients. A new pilot programme in Tamil Nadu is now testing whether vaccination can become another important part of that strategy. Kadayanallur in Tenkasi district has been selected for the pilot, marking an important step in India's efforts to explore dengue vaccination on the ground. AI Summary AI-generated summary, reviewed by editors A dengue vaccination pilot is underway in Kadayanallur, Tamil Nadu, testing the Japanese-developed Qdenga vaccine, approved for ages 4-60 in India, to evaluate its contribution to dengue prevention alongside existing strategies. The choice of Kadayanallur is not accidental. The town has reported a high incidence of dengue, making it a useful setting to study how vaccination works in a population that regularly faces the disease, according to a report in The Hindu. But the exercise will involve more than simply giving people the vaccine. Health officials will also have to track how many eligible residents take the doses, whether they return for the second shot, any side effects and, most importantly, whether dengue cases come down over time. What is the vaccine being used in Tamil Nadu? The vaccine being used in the Kadayanallur programme is Qdenga, developed by Japanese pharmaceutical company Takeda. Qdenga is a live-attenuated, tetravalent dengue vaccine. Put simply, it uses weakened forms of the dengue virus to train the immune system and is designed to provide protection against all four known dengue virus serotypes - DENV-1, DENV-2, DENV-3 and DENV-4. India's Central Drugs Standard Control Organisation approved Qdenga in July 2026 for people between four and 60 years of age. Also Read Tamil Music Composer Deva Dies At 75; Thenisai Thendral’ Was Known For Baashha, Annamalai The vaccination schedule involves two doses, with the second dose given three months after the first. This is not India's indigenous dengue vaccine The Tamil Nadu programme should not be confused with India's own dengue vaccine candidate. DengiAll, developed by Panacea Biotec in collaboration with the Indian Council of Medical Research (ICMR), is a separate vaccine. It entered Phase III clinical trials in August 2024, with 10,335 participants across 19 sites. DengiAll uses dengue virus strains licensed from the US National Institutes of Health (NIH). So, while both vaccines are aimed at tackling dengue, the vaccine being evaluated in the Kadayanallur pilot is the Japan-developed Qdenga. Why is dengue vaccination important? Dengue remains a recurring public health problem in India, particularly during periods when mosquito breeding conditions become favourable. The disease is mainly spread through the bite of infected Aedes mosquitoes. For many patients, it causes high fever, severe headache, muscle and joint pain, nausea and other symptoms. In more serious cases, dengue can lead to bleeding, plasma leakage, shock and organ complications. Recommended For You Regime Change Only Through Elections, Not Soros: Kiren Rijiju Warns Of Bangladesh, France-Like Unrest In India Rainfall, temperature, urban growth and stagnant water can all influence the spread of the disease. This makes dengue control difficult because eliminating mosquito breeding sites and protecting people from mosquito bites require sustained efforts. A vaccine, if shown to work effectively in the real-world Indian setting, could give health authorities another weapon in that fight. What will the Tamil Nadu pilot actually tell us? The number of people vaccinated will be only one part of the story. Authorities will need to look at how many people complete the two-dose schedule, what adverse events are reported and whether there is any change in laboratory-confirmed dengue infections. You May Also Like Anirudh Ravichander-Kavya Maran Wedding: Family Invites Vijay, Stalin, Greece Ceremony Planned For 11 November Hospitalisations will also be important. So will the overall cost of running a vaccination programme and whether the benefits justify expanding it to other dengue-prone areas. According to the launch report, two ICMR scientists will study the impact of vaccination in Kadayanallur. The findings could therefore have implications beyond one town in Tenkasi. If the programme produces encouraging results, dengue vaccination could gradually become part of a broader prevention strategy in India - alongside mosquito control and early treatment, rather than replacing them. As dengue continues to rise in Florida, some are worried about underreporting of cases - ABC News - Breaking News, Latest News and Videos
新闻简报Dengue cases are rising in Florida, with at least 190 confirmed locally acquired cases reported in six counties as of the week ending Sept. 19, 2026. Hillsborough County, which includes Tampa, has the highest number of cases at 170. Public health specialists are worried about underreporting, as many cases may go unnoticed due to mild symptoms or lack of testing. The Florida Department of Health (DOH) reported the state's first dengue death of the year, though details about the patient were not disclosed. The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) has acknowledged the "unprecedented" number of cases in the Tampa area and provided $800,000 in emergency funds to support the response. Three counties—Hillsborough, Pasco, and Pinellas—have declared local states of emergency to facilitate mosquito control efforts. The CDC also noted that climate change could increase the geographic range and severity of dengue, as Aedes aegypti mosquitoes are common in the U.S. and may lead to continued local spread.

ABC News Live Video Local Shows Good Morning America ShopGMA Interest Successfully Added We'll notify you here with news about Turn on desktop notifications for breaking stories about interest? OffOn Stream on As dengue continues to rise in Florida, some are worried about underreporting of cases At least 190 cases of locally acquired dengue have been confirmed in 6 counties. ByMary Kekatos September 30, 2026, 3:00 PM Share As cases of dengue fever continue to rise in Florida, public health specialists are worried the true number of infections may be greater. For the week ending Sept. 19, at least 190 cases of locally acquired dengue have been confirmed in the state, an increase of 38 cases from the week before, according to data from the Florida Department of Health (DOH). Hillsborough County, which includes Tampa, is reporting the highest number of cases at 170 followed by Miami-Dade, Orange, Palm Beach, Pasco and Pinellas counties, DOH data shows. Related What to know about dengue fever as Florida reports 1st death and cases rise "There's evidence of local transmission of dengue in several counties in Florida," Natalie Dean, an associate professor of biostatistics and informatics at Emory University's Rollins School of Public Health, told ABC News. "Dengue is not endemic to the United States. That means that it doesn't typically cause local transmission." She added, "There can be occasional outbreaks in the U.S. but this one is notable for its size." Last week, DOH reported the state's first death from dengue fever this year but did not provide any details about the patient including name, age, sex or residence. Close-up of a mosquito biting human skin. Joao Paulo Burini/Getty Images Three counties -- Hillsborough, Pasco and Pinellas -- declared local states of emergency last week. These allow local governments to waive normal procedures, access emergency resources and strengthen efforts to control mosquito populations. Pinellas County crews have been going door to door and spraying areas with high mosquito presence, according to local ABC News affiliate WFTS. "I don't recall a [period] of time when it's been this high in such a sort of concentrated area before," Cameron Wolfe, an infectious diseases specialist and a professor of medicine at Duke University School of Medicine, told ABC News. Wolfe said there may be an underestimate of the true number of cases in Florida. Cases of dengue can be mild, and people may recover on their own or never be tested. Because dengue can resemble a flu-like illness, patients may never suspect the source of their infection. Additionally, he said that reporting of confirmed cases to either local state departments or the CDC can lag. Popular Reads 'Dancing with the Stars' season 35: See who is eliminated 21 hours ago North Korea threatens to restart propaganda broadcasts, deploy more weapons at border Oct 19 Fisherman, 71, attacked and killed by 12-foot alligator alongside Florida lake Oct 17 "For every case that we know, there's going to be a bunch that we never find out about," Wolfe said. Related Increased risk of dengue virus infections in the US: CDC Dean added that people can be infected with dengue multiple times over the course of their life, but it's usually the second or later infection that is more severe, leading to further underreporting. "I always think of like the top of an iceberg," Dean said. "We're only capturing some of the some of the cases." The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) told ABC News in a statement on Tuesday that the agency is "closely tracking dengue activity" and the "unprecedented" number of cases in the Tampa area. The CDC also said they've provided $800,000 in emergency funds to help with the response. "Because the types of mosquitoes that spread dengue, Aedes aegypti, are common throughout many areas of the United States, continued local spread of dengue is possible," the CDC statement said. "The best way to prevent dengue is to protect yourself from mosquito bites by using EPA-registered insect repellant, wearing loose fitting clothes, and controlling mosquitoes in and around your home, such as dumping water." Related Dengue fever cases rise to 6,800 in US amid 2 new infections in Los Angeles: CDC Some research has shown that climate change could be expanding the geographic range and severity of dengue fever. Rising global temperature coupled with more rainfall and humidity -- leading to standing water pools that serve as breeding grounds for mosquitoes -- could increase the number of cases. "Global warming and climate change, in fact, leave places like Florida more wet and more capable of housing these mosquito vectors, so I wouldn't be surprised if this becomes a little more of a perpetual thing," Wolfe said. ABC News' Youri Benadjaoud contributed to this report. Short Sleeves and Work Outdoors Drove a Dengue Outbreak in Northern Yemen - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报一项发表在《BMC Infectious Diseases》上的病例对照研究确认了也门Amran省Harf Sufyan区的一次登革热暴发。此次暴发在2023年流行病学第39周达到高峰,与两个关键风险因素相关:穿着短袖衣物和户外工作。与不具有这些行为的人相比,这些暴露因素分别使感染风险增加了17倍和23倍。该研究强调了行为预防策略在登革热控制中的重要性,例如使用防护性衣物。此次暴发的发病率是0.9/100,可能对当地卫生基础设施造成压力,尤其是在登革热可能发展为重症登革热或登革热休克综合征的情况下。研究结果表明,在登革热暴发范围扩大的地区,公共卫生努力应将白天职业暴露和衣物覆盖作为首要的行为干预目标。

此次登革热暴发最早于2023年9月初被注意到,首例病例记录于流行病学第37周,高峰出现在第39周。调查时间为2023年9月5日至10月29日。

此次登革热暴发发生在也门Amran省Harf Sufyan区。研究涉及34例确认的登革热病例和68例对照者,其中男性占病例的58%,且30岁以下患者占43%。

一项发表在《BMC Infectious Diseases》上的病例对照研究确认了也门Amran省Harf Sufyan区的一次登革热暴发。研究确定穿着短袖衣物和户外工作是登革热感染的重要风险因素。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Short Sleeves and Work Outdoors Drove a Dengue Outbreak in Northern Yemen - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A dengue fever outbreak in Harf Sufyan district, Amran governorate, Yemen, was confirmed through a case-control study published in BMC Infectious Diseases. The outbreak, which peaked in epidemiological week 39 of 2023, was linked to two key risk factors: wearing short-sleeved clothing and working outdoors. These exposures increased the likelihood of infection by 17 and 23 times, respectively, compared to individuals not sharing these behaviors. The study highlights the importance of behavioral prevention strategies, such as using protective clothing, in dengue control. With an attack rate of 0.9 per 100, the outbreak could strain local health infrastructure, especially as dengue can progress to severe forms like dengue hemorrhagic fever or dengue shock syndrome. The findings suggest that public health efforts should focus on daytime occupational exposure and clothing coverage as first-line behavioral targets in regions with expanding dengue transmission.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Sunday, October 18, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Short Sleeves and Work Outdoors Drove a Dengue Outbreak in Northern Yemen by October 18, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 4 mins read 0 Short Sleeves and Work Outdoors Drove a Dengue Outbreak in Northern Yemen Check Symptoms Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Dengue fever has long been endemic in Yemen, but a cluster of suspected cases in a remote northern district has now given researchers a rare, detailed look at what actually fuels an outbreak in a fragile, conflict-affected setting. When surveillance officers in Amran governorate noticed a surge of suspected dengue in Harf Sufyan district in early September 2023, a rapid investigation team was dispatched within days. The resulting case-control study, published in BMC Infectious Diseases, confirms the outbreak and pinpoints two strikingly simple risk factors: wearing short-sleeved clothing and working outdoors. The investigation began on September 5, 2023, just one day after the surveillance officer in Amran governorate reported an increased number of suspected dengue fever cases in Harf Sufyan. A joint team from Yemen’s electronic Integrated Disease Early Warning System, known as eIDEWS, and the Rapid Response program traveled to the district to confirm whether an outbreak existed, determine its risk factors, and recommend control measures. Their work offers a technical template for outbreak response in one of the world’s most challenging public health environments. Discover more Study Biology Get Health Tips Donate to Research The team conducted a descriptive case-control study in Harf Sufyan from September 5 to October 29, 2023, using a 1:2 matching ratio. Cases were defined according to the standard World Health Organization case definition of dengue fever, while controls were any person living in the same area whose house was adjacent to the homes of cases and who had not developed dengue fever. Data were collected on individual, environmental, and behavioral risk factors. Attack rates, odds ratios, and confidence intervals were calculated, with a p-value below 0.05 considered statistically significant. Check Symptoms Discover more Check Symptoms Buy Maps Read History Learn Arabic Laboratory confirmation strengthened the descriptive picture. A total of 34 patients tested positive for dengue fever IgM antibodies, and 68 controls were enrolled. Males made up 58 percent of the confirmed cases, and 43 percent were younger than 30 years of age. The overall attack rate was 0.9 per 100 of the population. The first case fell in epidemiological week 37 of 2023, and the outbreak reached its peak in week 39, a compressed epidemic curve typical of explosive dengue transmission in a susceptible population. Multivariate analysis cut through the noise of dozens of candidate exposures. Only two factors remained significantly associated with dengue infection after adjustment: wearing shorts and T-shirts, which carried an odds ratio of 17 with a confidence interval of 4.8 to 60.1, and working, which carried an odds ratio of 23 with a confidence interval of 2.9 to 33.2. In practical terms, people who wore short-sleeved clothing were roughly 17 times more likely to be infected, and those who worked were roughly 23 times more likely, than their neighbors who did not share those exposures. Check Symptoms The biological logic behind these findings is straightforward. Dengue virus is transmitted by Aedes mosquitoes, principally Aedes aegypti, which bite during daytime hours and preferentially attack exposed skin. Short sleeves and T-shirts leave forearms and legs uncovered, maximizing the surface area available to a day-biting vector. Outdoor work places people in the open during the very hours when Aedes aegypti is most active, multiplying the number of potential infective bites. In Harf Sufyan, where many residents labor in fields and open-air workplaces, the combination of exposed skin and occupational daytime presence created ideal conditions for rapid viral amplification. The demographic profile of the outbreak adds context. With males comprising a majority of cases and nearly half of patients under 30, the epidemic pattern mirrors the working-age structure of the exposed population rather than any special vulnerability of children or the elderly. An attack rate of 0.9 per 100 may sound modest, but in a district with limited health infrastructure, even a small proportion of clinically ill patients can overwhelm primary care facilities, particularly when dengue can progress to severe dengue, dengue hemorrhagic fever, or dengue shock syndrome in a minority of infections. Discover more Track Outbreaks Find Treatments infection infections Infectious Diseases The authors conclude that dengue fever is emerging as an important disease in Harf Sufyan district of Amran governorate. Occupational exposure and wearing short-sleeved clothing were identified as the primary significant risk factors, a finding with immediate operational value. Because the protective behavior is as simple as covering more skin, the study recommends increasing community awareness about dengue infection, its mode of transmission, and control measures, along with the use of protective clothing. Such messaging can be delivered through existing community networks without costly equipment or pharmaceutical intervention. Check Symptoms The broader significance of the study extends beyond a single district. Yemen’s health system has been battered by years of conflict, and the eIDEWS and Rapid Response infrastructure demonstrated here shows that even under strain, rapid outbreak confirmation and analytic epidemiology remain feasible. The case-control design, executed with a 1:2 ratio and standard WHO case definitions, produced actionable odds ratios within weeks of the initial alert. For other districts in the Eastern Mediterranean Region where dengue is expanding, the Yemeni experience suggests that vector-borne risk assessment should focus on daytime occupational exposure and clothing coverage as first-line behavioral targets. For a virus with no specific antiviral treatment and a vaccine landscape still uneven in endemic countries, behavioral prevention remains the backbone of dengue control. The Harf Sufyan outbreak, confirmed through 34 IgM-positive cases and dissected through multivariate analysis, distilled that backbone into two measurable exposures. As dengue continues its global expansion into new territories, the lesson from northern Yemen is that the fight against the virus can begin with something as ordinary as a long-sleeved shirt and as fundamental as recognizing who, in a community, faces the greatest daytime exposure to the mosquitoes that carry it. The outbreak occurred in Harf Sufyan district, Amran governorate, Yemen. The study involved 34 confirmed dengue fever cases and 68 controls, with males making up 58% of the cases and 43% of patients under 30 years of age.
Event
A dengue fever outbreak was confirmed in Harf Sufyan district, Amran governorate, Yemen, through a case-control study published in BMC Infectious Diseases. The study identified wearing short-sleeved clothing and working outdoors as significant risk factors for dengue infection.
Scale, impact and risk
The overall attack rate was 0.9 per 100 of the population. The outbreak reached its peak in epidemiological week 39, and the study found that wearing short sleeves and working outdoors were significantly associated with dengue infection, with odds ratios of 17 and 23, respectively. The outbreak could overwhelm primary care facilities due to the limited health infrastructure in the region.
Response, status and uncertainty
A joint team from Yemen’s eIDEWS and Rapid Response program confirmed the outbreak, identified risk factors, and recommended community awareness campaigns about dengue infection, its transmission, and control measures, including the use of protective clothing. The study provides a technical template for outbreak response in conflict-affected settings.

登革热传播蚊虫提示加纳北部黄热病高风险暴发区域

Dengue-Carrying Mosquitoes Signal High Yellow Fever Risk in Northern Ghana Outbreak Zone
新闻简报2026年10月,针对加纳萨瓦纳地区2021年黄热病暴发的昆虫学调查发现,蚊虫繁殖活动频繁,但所检测的蚊虫对黄热病病毒呈阴性。由诺格奇纪念医学研究所的约瑟夫·哈罗德·尼亚尔科·奥塞伊领导的研究发现,共检查了12,264个潜在繁殖容器,其中1.26%含有埃及伊蚊幼虫和蛹。在调查的1,001户家庭中,12.99%的家庭存在这些蚊虫,表明该地区存在显著的虫媒病毒传播传播风险。卫生部门报告了202例疑似病例、70例确诊病例和35例死亡,确诊病例的病死率为50%。该暴发引发了紧急的跨学科应对措施,包括使用BG-Sentinel陷阱、口腔吸管和Prokopack吸管收集成蚊。研究结果突显了埃及伊蚊formosus在森林循环中的作用,以及农村地区现代储水方式所带来的风险。该研究强调了在抗病毒药物监测覆盖有限的地区,需要改进监测和控制策略。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究是对加纳萨瓦纳地区2021年黄热病暴发的响应,研究于2021年进行,研究成果于2026年10月16日发表。
地点与对象
该暴发发生在加纳萨瓦纳地区,该地区自2019年起成为新设立的行政区域。受影响的社区包括蚊虫密集的住户和聚居地,尤其是与森林循环相关的埃及伊蚊formosus。
事件
为评估黄热病等虫媒病毒传播在加纳萨瓦纳地区的风险,进行了广泛的昆虫学调查。研究发现,蚊虫种群数量超过了世界卫生组织暴发的阈值,表明病毒传播的高潜力,但所检测的蚊虫对黄热病病毒呈阴性。
规模、影响与风险
卫生部门记录了202例疑似病例,其中70例经实验室确诊,35例死亡。疑似病例的病死率为17%,确诊病例的病死率为50%。研究确定了12,264个繁殖容器,其中1.26%含有埃及伊蚊幼虫和蛹,1,001户家庭中有12.99%的家庭存在这些蚊虫。
应对、状态与不确定性
该调查是针对暴发的跨学科应对措施的一部分。研究记录了昆虫学发现及其对病毒监测和控制的潜在影响。然而,所检测的蚊虫对黄热病病毒呈阴性,研究重点在于风险因素,而非确认活跃的传播。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Dengue-Carrying Mosquitoes Signal High Yellow Fever Risk in Northern Ghana Outbreak Zone

News Brief
In October 2026, an entomological investigation into the 2021 yellow fever outbreak in Ghana’s Savannah Region revealed high mosquito breeding activity, though the mosquitoes tested negative for the yellow fever virus. The study, led by Joseph Harold Nyarko Osei of the Noguchi Memorial Institute for Medical Research, found that 12,264 potential breeding containers were examined, with 1.26% being positive for Aedes larvae and pupae. Of the 1,001 houses surveyed, 12.99% harbored these mosquitoes, indicating a significant risk for arbovirus transmission. Health authorities reported 202 suspected cases, 70 confirmed, and 35 deaths, with a case fatality rate of 50% among confirmed cases. The outbreak prompted an urgent, multidisciplinary response, including the use of BG-Sentinel traps, oral aspirators, and Prokopack aspirators to collect adult mosquitoes. The findings highlight the role of Aedes aegypti formosus in the sylvatic cycle and the risk posed by modern water storage practices in rural areas. The study underscores the need for improved surveillance and containment strategies in regions with limited vaccination coverage.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, October 16, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Dengue-Carrying Mosquitoes Signal High Yellow Fever Risk in Northern Ghana Outbreak Zone by October 16, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 Dengue-Carrying Mosquitoes Signal High Yellow Fever Risk in Northern Ghana Outbreak Zone Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram An extensive entomological investigation into the 2021 yellow fever outbreak in Ghana’s Savannah Region has revealed a landscape primed for arbovirus transmission, even though the mosquitoes collected during the response tested negative for the virus itself. The study, published in PLOS Global Public Health by a team of Ghanaian researchers led by Joseph Harold Nyarko Osei of the Noguchi Memorial Institute for Medical Research, documents dense populations of Aedes aegypti mosquitoes, an overwhelming dominance of the sylvatic subspecies Aedes aegypti formosus, and container-breeding conditions that placed the affected communities squarely above World Health Organization outbreak thresholds. The findings carry significant implications for how West African nations monitor and contain a virus that continues to erupt unpredictably across the continent. The outbreak that prompted the investigation was severe by any measure. Health authorities recorded 202 suspected cases, of which 70 were laboratory confirmed, with 35 deaths attributed to the disease. The case fatality rate among suspected cases stood at 17 percent, but among confirmed cases it reached a startling 50 percent, meaning that half of the people whose infections were confirmed by testing did not survive. Such mortality is consistent with the clinical course of yellow fever, a viral hemorrhagic disease that progresses from an initial febrile phase, in many severe cases, to a toxic phase marked by jaundice, organ failure, internal bleeding, and shock. The Savannah Region, one of Ghana’s newest administrative areas carved from the former Northern Region in 2019, had not previously been regarded as a yellow fever hotspot, and the sudden eruption of cases demanded an urgent, multidisciplinary response. A team assembled specifically for its expertise in yellow fever outbreak response was tasked with assessing the extent of vector activity and identifying practical measures to contain or control the rapid spread of the virus. The entomological component of that response formed the backbone of the published study. In arbovirus epidemiology, entomological investigations serve a dual purpose: they quantify the density and distribution of competent vectors, which determines the force of transmission, and they attempt to isolate virus from field-caught mosquitoes, which provides direct evidence of ongoing transmission. The team employed a combination of larval surveys and adult mosquito collections designed to capture the full picture of vector abundance across the affected communities. The larval survey was exhaustive. Investigators examined 12,264 potential breeding containers in and around houses, a scale of surveillance that speaks to the density of water-holding receptacles in the surveyed settlements. From these containers, they recovered 3,885 larvae and 1,186 pupae distributed across 173 positive containers, yielding a container positivity rate of 1.26 percent. Of the 1,001 houses surveyed, 130 harbored larvae or pupae, a house positivity rate of 12.99 percent. These raw counts were converted into the classical World Health Organization larval indices: the House Index, the Container Index, the Breteau Index, and the Pupal Demographic Index. Collectively, these metrics assess the risk of arbovirus transmission, with established thresholds separating low-risk from high-risk conditions. The indices recorded in the Savannah Region exceeded the levels generally regarded as indicative of epidemic risk, meaning that the density of Aedes breeding was sufficient to sustain and amplify viral transmission had competent, infected mosquitoes been present in sufficient numbers. Morphological identification of the immature stages followed standard taxonomic criteria. The larvae were recognized by their short and stout siphons, the breathing tubes characteristic of Aedes species, which distinguish them from the longer, more slender siphons of Culex larvae. Pupae were identified by the short but distinctly serrated spicules along the paddle fringe and by the branched, fanlike seta 9-VIII bearing six to seven branches, anatomical characters that allow entomologists to separate Aedes pupae from those of other medically important culicines. Such morphological work remains the practical foundation of outbreak entomology in resource-limited settings, where molecular identification of every specimen is rarely feasible and where rapid, accurate triage of mosquitoes determines how sampling proceeds. The composition of the breeding containers offered a revealing portrait of peridomestic risk. Plastic containers accounted for the largest share, 6,529 receptacles or 46.95 percent of those examined, followed by metallic containers at 6,024, or 43.32 percent. Clay jars contributed 753 receptacles, or 5.42 percent, while discarded tires made up 565, or 4.06 percent, and open wells the remaining 34, or 0.24 percent. This distribution reflects the water storage practices common in rural northern Ghana, where households store water in a variety of containers that, if left uncovered or allowed to collect rainwater, become ideal developmental sites for Aedes aegypti. Plastic and metallic containers dominate the profile, underscoring how modern materials have multiplied breeding opportunities in communities where water supply is intermittent and storage is essential. Discarded tires, long recognized as among the most productive Aedes habitats worldwide, added another layer of risk. Adult mosquito collections added a crucial dimension to the assessment. The team deployed BG-Sentinel traps, a design that uses visual cues and chemical attractants to lure host-seeking Aedes, and supplemented these with oral aspirators, hand-held suction devices that collect mosquitoes resting on walls and other surfaces, and Prokopack aspirators, motorized backpack units that allow rapid collection from elevated indoor and outdoor resting sites. A total of 1,571 adult mosquitoes were identified in the collections. The species composition was striking: 35 were Aedes aegypti aegypti, the domesticated subspecies that prefers to feed on humans and breeds in human-made containers, while 619 were Aedes aegypti formosus, the wild, sylvatic form that typically feeds on non-human hosts in forest cycles. The remaining 917 specimens were Culex species, vectors of filariasis and West Nile virus but not primary yellow fever vectors. Outdoor collections overwhelmingly outnumbered indoor ones, accounting for 86.4 percent of the adult catch against 13.6 percent indoors. The dominance of Aedes aegypti formosus is arguably the study’s most significant entomological finding. The formosus subspecies is the ancestral, feral form of the yellow fever vector, adapted to breeding in tree holes and feeding on primates and other wild animals in the sylvatic cycle, the forest transmission loop in which the yellow fever virus circulates between monkeys and canopy-dwelling mosquitoes. Yet formosus populations in West Africa are known to occupy a transitional ecological role: they colonize peridomestic containers, feed opportunistically on humans when wild hosts are scarce or when humans encroach on forest edges, and can therefore act as bridge vectors, carrying virus from sylvatic reservoirs into human populations. The authors suggest that this subspecies may have been the driver of the outbreak and of yellow fever transmission in the Savannah Region, a hypothesis consistent with the pattern seen in several recent African epidemics where spillover from sylvatic cycles into unvaccinated rural communities ignited explosive human-to-mosquito-to-human amplification. Despite the abundance of vectors, the virological screening came back empty. All mosquitoes collected during the investigation were tested by real-time reverse-transcription polymerase chain reaction for four arboviruses: yellow fever, dengue, chikungunya, and Zika. The assay’s detection limit of fewer than 10 RNA copies per reaction meant that even very low-level infections would likely have been detected. No arbovirus was found in any specimen. This negative result does not contradict the outbreak; rather, it reflects the practical realities of arbovirus field investigation. Adult mosquito collections typically take place after the peak of transmission, once control measures, case isolation, and insecticide spraying have reduced both vector density and infection prevalence. Infection rates in wild mosquito populations during endemic transmission are often on the order of one infected individual per thousand or fewer, so capturing an infected mosquito requires collecting very large numbers of adults, something a short outbreak-response survey can rarely achieve. The absence of virus in the sampled mosquitoes therefore reinforces, rather than undermines, the study’s central conclusion drawn from the vector indices: that conditions in the region posed a high risk of arbovirus transmission. The wider context sharpens the public health urgency of these findings. Yellow fever, dengue, Zika, and chikungunya are among the most consequential emerging and re-emerging infectious diseases on the planet, and by recent estimates half the world’s population lives at risk of infection by the viruses that mosquitoes of the Aedes complex transmit. Recent African epidemics have confirmed 19,569 cases with 820 deaths, a toll that underscores both the persistence of sylvatic yellow fever reservoirs and the vulnerability of communities with incomplete vaccination coverage. The Savannah Region outbreak, with its 50 percent case fatality rate among confirmed cases, fits a familiar pattern: a rural population with low immunity, abundant vectors, and proximity to sylvatic transmission cycles. The study’s authors argue that the entomological evidence, dominated by Aedes aegypti formosus breeding above epidemic thresholds, poses a continuing public health threat that demands robust Aedes mosquito surveillance systems and sustained vector control strategies, including source reduction of plastic and metallic water-storage containers, larviciding of productive habitats, and vaccination campaigns to close the immunity gap. Without such measures, the region remains primed for the next spillover. Time
The study was conducted in response to the 2021 yellow fever outbreak in the Savannah Region of Ghana, with the publication date being October 16, 2026. The investigation was carried out in 2021, and the findings were published in 2026.
Location and population
The outbreak occurred in the Savannah Region of Ghana, a newly formed administrative area since 2019. The affected communities included households and settlements where Aedes aegypti mosquitoes were prevalent, particularly Aedes aegypti formosus, which is associated with sylvatic cycles.
Event
An extensive entomological investigation was conducted to assess the risk of arbovirus transmission, including yellow fever, in the Savannah Region of Ghana. The study found that the mosquito populations were above WHO outbreak thresholds, indicating a high potential for viral spread, though the mosquitoes tested negative for yellow fever virus.
Scale, impact and risk
Health authorities recorded 202 suspected cases, with 70 laboratory-confirmed cases and 35 deaths. The case fatality rate was 17% among suspected cases and 50% among confirmed cases. The study identified 12,264 breeding containers, with 1.26% being positive for Aedes larvae and pupae, and 12.99% of houses harboring these mosquitoes.
Response, status and uncertainty
The investigation was part of a multidisciplinary response to the outbreak. The study documented the entomological findings and their implications for monitoring and containing the virus. However, the mosquitoes tested negative for yellow fever virus, and the study focused on the risk factors rather than confirming active transmission.

登革热死亡病例隐藏在未确认病例中,菲律宾13年监测研究揭示 - Bioengineer.org

Dengue Deaths Hide in Unconfirmed Cases, 13-Year Philippine Surveillance Study Reveals - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报A 13-year surveillance study in Davao City, Philippines, revealed a striking paradox in dengue monitoring: while laboratory-confirmed cases increased, the majority of dengue deaths occurred among unconfirmed cases. The study, published in Frontiers in Tropical Diseases, analyzed 81,075 dengue notifications from 2012 to 2024, finding that suspected cases dominated the records, accounting for nearly 75% of all notifications. The three-tier classification system—suspected, probable, and confirmed—produced divergent trends, with confirmed cases showing a significant upward trend (p = 0.003) and suspected cases showing a slight decline. Mortality rates were highest among probable cases (0.85%) and confirmed cases (0.67%), with suspected cases having the lowest (0.26%). The authors attribute the rise in confirmed cases to improved diagnostic testing, expanded laboratory capacity, and enhanced surveillance practices. The study also noted that the decline in dengue notifications during the COVID-19 pandemic was likely due to movement restrictions and altered healthcare-seeking behavior, but the increase in confirmed cases persisted beyond the pandemic, indicating broader trends in dengue transmission and detection.

该研究分析了达沃市登革热监测数据,时间范围为2012年1月至2024年12月,发表日期为2026年10月16日。时间分析显示,在研究期间确认登革热发病率呈现上升趋势,2024年观察到显著增加。
地点与对象
该研究聚焦于菲律宾达沃市,分析了81,075例登革热通报。受影响的人群包括所有年龄段的个体,其中15岁以下儿童占疑似、可能和确认病例的大部分比例。
事件
一项在菲律宾达沃市进行的13年监测研究揭示,尽管实验室确认的登革热病例数量增加,但大多数登革热死亡病例发生在未确认病例中。该研究强调了病例分类对解读登革热趋势和死亡率的影响。
规模、影响与风险
In 2024, over 13 million dengue cases and more than 8,700 dengue-related deaths were reported globally. In Davao City, suspected cases dominated the surveillance record, accounting for nearly 75% of all notifications. The cumulative case fatality rate was highest for probable cases (0.85%) and confirmed cases (0.67%), with suspected cases having the lowest (0.26%).
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究使用了官方全市监测数据库和PIDSR系统进行。作者强调,实验室确认的登革热发病率的上升趋势在疫情之后仍然明显,表明这种模式并非仅由疫情因素造成。研究的发现基于回顾性观察数据。

Dengue Deaths Hide in Unconfirmed Cases, 13-Year Philippine Surveillance Study Reveals - Bioengineer.org

A 13-year surveillance study in Davao City, Philippines, revealed a striking paradox in dengue monitoring: while laboratory-confirmed cases increased, the majority of dengue deaths occurred among unconfirmed cases. The study, published in Frontiers in Tropical Diseases, analyzed 81,075 dengue notifications from 2012 to 2024, finding that suspected cases dominated the records, accounting for nearly 75% of all notifications. The three-tier classification system—suspected, probable, and confirmed—produced divergent trends, with confirmed cases showing a significant upward trend (p = 0.003) and suspected cases showing a slight decline. Mortality rates were highest among probable cases (0.85%) and confirmed cases (0.67%), with suspected cases having the lowest (0.26%). The authors attribute the rise in confirmed cases to improved diagnostic testing, expanded laboratory capacity, and enhanced surveillance practices. The study also noted that the decline in dengue notifications during the COVID-19 pandemic was likely due to movement restrictions and altered healthcare-seeking behavior, but the increase in confirmed cases persisted beyond the pandemic, indicating broader trends in dengue transmission and detection.
