HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, October 16, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Dengue Deaths Hide in Unconfirmed Cases, 13-Year Philippine Surveillance Study Reveals by October 16, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 5 mins read 0 Dengue Deaths Hide in Unconfirmed Cases, 13-Year Philippine Surveillance Study Reveals Check Symptoms Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A sweeping 13-year analysis of dengue surveillance data from Davao City, Philippines, has revealed a striking paradox at the heart of routine disease monitoring: while laboratory-confirmed dengue cases rose significantly over time, the vast majority of recorded dengue deaths occurred among patients who were never laboratory-confirmed at all. The study, published in Frontiers in Tropical Diseases, examined 81,075 dengue notifications reported through the Philippine Integrated Disease Surveillance and Response (PIDSR) system between January 2012 and December 2024, and found that the three surveillance case classifications—suspected, probable, and confirmed—each told a distinctly different story about the epidemic unfolding in this major southern Philippine city. Dengue remains one of the most formidable public health challenges in tropical and subtropical regions. The mosquito-borne viral disease ranges in clinical presentation from a self-limiting febrile illness to severe disease marked by plasma leakage, hemorrhage, organ dysfunction, and death. In 2024 alone, more than 13 million dengue cases and over 8,700 dengue-related deaths were reported globally. The Philippines ranks among the most affected countries in Southeast Asia, where cyclical outbreaks continue to strain healthcare and public health systems. Yet interpreting long-term dengue trends has always been complicated by changes in case definitions, reporting practices, and the availability of laboratory confirmation—factors that can reshape the apparent epidemiological landscape without any true change in transmission. The research team, led by Alfredo A. Hinay Jr. of the University of the Immaculate Conception in Davao, conducted a retrospective observational study using the official citywide surveillance database coordinated by the City Epidemiology and Surveillance Unit. In the Philippine system, dengue is a nationally notifiable disease, and healthcare providers must report cases meeting national surveillance definitions. The three-tier classification system distinguishes suspected cases—previously healthy individuals with acute febrile illness of two to seven days duration and clinical signs consistent with dengue—from probable cases, which additionally show a positive dengue IgM antibody test, and confirmed cases, which require positive results from viral culture isolation, polymerase chain reaction (PCR), or dengue NS1 antigen testing. Each tier represents a different level of diagnostic certainty, and each captured a different slice of the epidemic. Donate to Research Discover more Check Symptoms Find Grief Support deaths Deaths death Of the 81,075 notifications analyzed after data cleaning, 59,021 were classified as suspected cases, 20,559 as probable cases, and only 1,495 as confirmed cases. Suspected cases therefore dominated the surveillance record throughout the entire 13-year period, accounting for nearly three-quarters of all notifications. Demographically, the burden fell hardest on children: individuals younger than 15 years represented 59.4 percent of suspected cases, 61.4 percent of probable cases, and 62.7 percent of confirmed cases. Males comprised a slightly higher proportion of notifications than females across all classifications, ranging from 51.2 to 52.2 percent. Hospital admission was reported in more than 90 percent of notifications across every classification, indicating that patients classified at all levels of diagnostic certainty received comparable clinical attention within the healthcare system. Buy First Aid Kits The temporal analysis, performed using the Mann–Kendall trend test—a non-parametric method for detecting monotonic trends in time-series data that does not require assumptions of normality—produced the study’s most consequential finding. Confirmed dengue incidence increased significantly over the study period (p = 0.003), while probable dengue incidence remained stable with no significant trend (p = 0.855), and suspected dengue incidence showed a decreasing, though not statistically significant, tendency (p = 0.059). In other words, the same city, the same virus, and the same surveillance system produced three divergent trend lines depending solely on how cases were classified. Had the analysis been restricted to laboratory-confirmed cases alone, observers would have concluded that dengue was steadily worsening; had it relied on suspected cases, the conclusion might have been one of gradual improvement. Discover more Buy First Aid Kits Read Security News Protect Privacy The increasing trend in confirmed dengue incidence proved remarkably consistent across every stratum examined. All three congressional districts of Davao City showed significant upward trends—Congressional District I (p = 0.005), Congressional District II (p = 0.016), and Congressional District III (p = 0.002)—and the increase was significant among both children under 15 (p = 0.005) and adults aged 15 or older (p = 0.014), as well as among both females (p = 0.005) and males (p = 0.003). This uniformity suggests that the rise in confirmed cases was not confined to particular neighborhoods or demographic groups but reflected a broad, citywide phenomenon. The authors attribute such increases in other dengue-endemic settings to a combination of changing transmission dynamics, expanded availability of diagnostic testing, improvements in laboratory capacity, and enhanced surveillance practices. Notably, the Department of Health only formally recognized positive NS1 antigen test results as a criterion for laboratory-confirmed dengue in 2024, so its influence on the long-term trend is likely limited, though it may have shaped the distribution of classifications in the final year. Check Symptoms The COVID-19 pandemic casts a long shadow over any interpretation of these trends. Consistent with reports from other dengue-endemic countries, dengue notifications in Davao City declined during the pandemic, likely reflecting movement restrictions, school closures, altered healthcare-seeking behavior, and disruptions to routine surveillance and healthcare services. The subsequent increase following the relaxation of public health measures may represent a post-pandemic rebound driven by resumed population mobility, renewed opportunities for virus transmission, and improved case detection. However, the authors emphasize that the increasing trend in laboratory-confirmed dengue remained evident beyond the immediate post-pandemic period, suggesting the pattern cannot be explained solely by pandemic effects. Perhaps the most sobering finding concerns mortality. Dengue mortality incidence showed no significant temporal trends in any classification—suspected (p = 0.089), probable (p = 0.242), or confirmed (p = 0.282)—yet the distribution of deaths across classifications was deeply asymmetric. Probable cases exhibited the highest cumulative case fatality rate at 0.85 percent (rising to 0.89 percent among admitted patients), followed by confirmed cases at 0.67 percent, and suspected cases at just 0.26 percent. Because suspected and probable cases vastly outnumbered confirmed ones, they accounted for the overwhelming majority of deaths recorded among reported dengue notifications. Among non-admitted patients, case fatality rates were low across all classifications, and no deaths were recorded among non-admitted confirmed notifications. The pattern suggests that diagnostic certainty and clinical severity do not necessarily align: patients may die of clinically compatible dengue without ever receiving the laboratory test that would place them in the confirmed category. The authors caution that routine passive surveillance systems carry inherent biases, including underreporting, delayed reporting, and misclassification. Patients with more severe presentations may have been more likely to undergo confirmatory testing and thus be classified as probable or confirmed, while those with milder illness or limited access to laboratory services may have remained classified as suspected. The study also could not clinically validate deaths recorded within the surveillance system, evaluate alternative diagnoses, or determine the extent to which individual deaths were directly attributable to dengue infection. Without an external reference standard, surveillance performance measures such as sensitivity and positive predictive value could not be assessed. These limitations temper but do not erase the central conclusion. For low- and middle-income countries, where dengue surveillance systems frequently operate under constraints related to diagnostic availability and laboratory capacity, the implications are substantial. Analyses restricted exclusively to confirmed cases may underestimate severe outcomes recorded within routine surveillance systems, since probable and suspected cases accounted for most deaths despite stable incidence trends over time. The authors argue that surveillance classifications beyond laboratory-confirmed dengue continue to provide meaningful epidemiological information about disease burden and severe outcomes in resource-constrained settings. As dengue continues its global expansion driven by urbanization, population growth, climate variability, and expanding mosquito habitats, the Davao City experience offers a cautionary lesson: the picture of an epidemic depends profoundly on where in the diagnostic pipeline one chooses to look, and public health decisions built on a single classification may miss the deaths that matter most. IgM抗体抑制寨卡病毒和登革热增强作用——Bioengineer.org

IgM antibodies curb Zika and dengue enhancement in laboratory studies - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报一项于2026年10月20日由Bioengineer.org发表的研究报告称,一种从寨卡病毒感染孕妇体内分离出的重组IgM单克隆抗体DH1017.IgM,在实验室检测中减少了寨卡病毒的抗体依赖性增强(ADE)作用。该抗体在与一系列IgG抗体的对比测试中显示出显著的ADE减少效果,平均减少了2.7倍。研究还测试了第二种IgM抗体C8.IgM,其对登革病毒型病毒也表现出类似效果。然而,该研究并未评估其在动物或人体中的保护作用,这些发现的临床意义尚不明确。研究结果表明,IgM抗体可能在减少ADE方面具有独特作用,且不依赖于中和作用,但需要进一步研究以确认其在实际场景中的有效性。该研究涉及感染了SJRP/2016-184或尼加拉瓜/2016型寨卡病毒的参与者,其中七人接受了安慰剂。这些发现指出了针对黄病毒属病毒ADE管理的潜在治疗途径,但并未提供预防重症登革热或寨卡病的证据。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于2026年10月20日完成并发布,发布日期为2026年10月20日。

该研究使用寨卡病毒和登革病毒型病毒进行实验室检测,参与者感染了SJRP/2016-184或尼加拉瓜/2016型寨卡病毒,其中七人接受了安慰剂。

一种重组IgM单克隆抗体DH1017.IgM从感染寨卡病毒的孕妇体内分离出来,并在实验室检测中发现其能减少寨卡病毒感染的抗体依赖性增强(ADE)作用,包括在低于病毒中和所需浓度的情况下。研究还测试了第二种IgM抗体C8.IgM,其对登革病毒型病毒表现出类似效果。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

IgM antibodies curb Zika and dengue enhancement in laboratory studies - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A study published on October 20, 2026, by Bioengineer.org reports that a recombinant IgM monoclonal antibody, DH1017.IgM, isolated from a pregnant woman with Zika infection, reduced antibody-dependent enhancement (ADE) of Zika virus in laboratory assays. The antibody was tested against a panel of IgG antibodies and showed a significant reduction in ADE, with an average of 2.7-fold decrease. The study also examined a second IgM antibody, C8.IgM, which demonstrated similar effects against dengue virus serotypes. However, the research did not assess protection in animals or humans, and the clinical significance of these findings remains unclear. The results suggest that IgM antibodies may have a distinct role in reducing ADE, independent of neutralization, but further studies are needed to confirm their efficacy in real-world scenarios. The study involved participants infected with either the SJRP/2016-184 or Nicaragua/2016 Zika strain, with seven individuals receiving placebo. The findings highlight a potential therapeutic avenue for managing ADE in flaviviruses but do not provide evidence for preventing severe dengue or Zika disease.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Tuesday, October 20, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Technology IgM antibodies curb Zika and dengue enhancement in laboratory studies by October 20, 2026 in Technology Reading Time: 5 mins read 0 IgM antibodies curb Zika and dengue enhancement in laboratory studies Study Biology Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A recombinant IgM monoclonal antibody reduced antibody-dependent enhancement (ADE) of Zika virus infection across a panel of IgG antibodies in laboratory assays, including at concentrations below those required for viral neutralization. A second pentameric IgM antibody produced a similar, though less uniform, effect against Zika virus and all four dengue virus serotypes. The findings indicate that reducing IgG-driven enhancement may be a function of IgM antibodies that is partly distinct from neutralization, but the experiments did not test protection in animals or people. ADE occurs when antibodies increase rather than reduce viral infection by promoting uptake of virus particles into Fc-gamma receptor-bearing cells. The phenomenon is well documented in dengue and has been implicated in severe disease during secondary infections. It has also been observed in laboratory studies of Zika virus, whose E-glycoprotein antibodies can enhance infection of monocytes, macrophages and other cells. The clinical importance of enhancement during Zika infection remains uncertain, although evidence from pregnant non-human primates, mice and ex vivo human placentas has led researchers to consider whether it could contribute to placental infection and vertical transmission. Study Biology For the current study, investigators used DH1017.IgM, a native pentameric IgM antibody isolated 71 days after symptom onset from a pregnant woman who had Zika infection and subsequently delivered a healthy baby. The antibody recognizes a discontinuous quaternary epitope on the Zika virus envelope E-protein dimer, spanning domain II and the interface between domains I and II. Its potent neutralization depends on its multimeric structure. Researchers proposed two possible binding arrangements: an umbrella-like configuration in which the pentamer contacts several E dimers on one virion, and a planar configuration in which it cross-links multiple virions. Earlier work estimated that only three such IgM molecules could bind simultaneously to one virion in the umbrella-like arrangement, leaving part of the recognized epitope space available to other antibodies. Study Biology Discover more Study Biology Check Symptoms Read Med Journals Compare Lab Gear Bioengineering careers guide The researchers first examined whether IgG monoclonal antibodies and DH1017.IgM would compete for binding to intact Zika virus particles. A panel of 14 characterized IgG antibodies targeted several regions of the envelope protein, including domain I, domain II, domain III, the fusion loop, the domain I/III linker and the E-dimer epitope 1. Two antibodies were excluded from reciprocal competition assays because they could not compete with their own labeled versions. The competition was incomplete in both directions. Some IgG antibodies displaced DH1017.IgM, while DH1017.IgM efficiently outcompeted several others, but even at saturating concentrations it generally did not eliminate IgG binding. Six of the 12 antibodies tested reduced DH1017.IgM binding by more than 75%, whereas only three blocked more than 75% of IgG binding with DH1017.IgM. This partial overlap is consistent with the large footprint of multivalent IgM and suggests that it can alter how many IgG molecules bind a virion without occupying every potentially accessible epitope. Study Biology These binding effects did not come at the expense of neutralization. When DH1017.IgM was combined with neutralizing or non-neutralizing Zika IgG antibodies in assays using GFP-expressing Zika reporter virus particles, its expected neutralizing activity remained intact. Combinations of DH1017.IgM with neutralizing IgG antibodies also tracked the increasing neutralization produced by the IgG alone. A control IgM antibody that did not recognize Zika did not alter the IgG neutralization profiles. The results therefore indicate that the cross-competition between IgM and IgG did not produce an obvious detrimental effect on either antibody class’s capacity to reduce viral infectivity. Discover more Earth’s Earth Science The more pronounced effect appeared in assays of ADE, in which antibodies were tested with Fc-gamma receptor-expressing K562 cells. At a fixed concentration corresponding to its reporter-virus neutralization midpoint, DH1017.IgM lowered peak IgG-mediated enhancement by an average of 2.7-fold, with reductions ranging from 1.8- to 3.8-fold. It did not simply shift the concentration at which peak enhancement occurred. Reversing the assay design and holding each IgG antibody at its peak enhancement concentration showed that DH1017.IgM reduced enhancement in a dose-dependent manner. Concentrations of 100 nanograms per milliliter or lower completely abrogated enhancement across the tested antibodies, regardless of which envelope epitope they recognized. The concentration required to reduce enhancement by 50% had a mean of 2.1 nanograms per milliliter and ranged from 0.4 to 9.1 nanograms per milliliter. Comparisons with neutralization potency showed that the enhancement-reducing action was not simply a consequence of virus neutralization. For most antibodies tested, DH1017.IgM reduced enhancement at sub-neutralizing concentrations, defined in the study by a ratio of the 50% enhancement-reduction concentration to the 50% reporter-virus neutralization concentration of no more than 0.75. Ratios ranged from 0.18 for the E-dimer epitope 1 antibodies C8 and C10 to 0.68 for the domain II antibody MZ20. The fusion-loop antibody 2A10G6 narrowly missed the threshold at 0.78 and showed substantial variation between replicates. A second recombinant IgM, C8.IgM, also reduced IgG-driven Zika enhancement in a dose-dependent fashion and did so at sub-neutralizing concentrations for six of 11 tested antibodies. Its profile differed from that of DH1017.IgM, showing that the effect depends on the particular IgM–IgG pairing rather than being a uniform property of all IgM antibodies. Study Biology Discover more Bioengineering News Subscription Bioengineering Career Guide Learn Engineering Track Biotech Tour Colleges Because C8 also recognizes dengue virus, the investigators extended its testing to dengue reporter virus particles representing serotypes 1 through 4. C8.IgM reduced enhancement mediated by six tested IgG antibodies for every dengue serotype, although the potency varied with both the IgG antibody and the serotype. It reduced enhancement at sub-neutralizing concentrations in 16 of 24 antibody–serotype combinations. This pattern was consistent for three antibodies—MZ20, C10 and the IgG version of C8—across all four serotypes. The antibody was generally less effective against IgG antibodies targeting the fusion loop. For example, the enhancement-reduction to neutralization ratio for 4G2 ranged from 1.2 for dengue 1 to 5.11 for dengue 2, indicating that concentrations sufficient to neutralize the virus were required to substantially reduce enhancement in those combinations. DH1017.IgM was also tested against unfractionated sera from a controlled human Zika infection study. Participants received either the SJRP/2016-184 or Nicaragua/2016 Zika strain, with nine and 10 infected participants respectively, while seven additional individuals received placebo. At 90 days after infection, sera from all 19 infected participants produced measurable enhancement of a heterologous Zika strain in vitro, whereas placebo sera did not. Adding DH1017.IgM reduced serum-mediated enhancement in a dose-dependent manner. The mean concentration required for 50% reduction was 6.2 nanograms per milliliter for the SJRP cohort and 6.0 nanograms per milliliter for the Nicaragua cohort, with no statistically significant difference between groups. DH1017.IgM reduced enhancement at sub-neutralizing concentrations in 16 of 19 sera, or 84.2% of the tested samples. The authors interpret the findings as evidence that IgM antibodies can mitigate IgG-mediated ADE through mechanisms that are probably multifactorial. Altered antibody-binding stoichiometry may contribute, but the strongest reductions also occurred with IgG antibodies whose epitopes were not efficiently displaced by DH1017.IgM, implying that additional mechanisms remain to be defined. The results support further investigation of multivalent IgM biologics for Zika and dengue, including possible effects on placental infection and vertical transmission. They do not establish clinical benefit. All experiments were conducted in vitro or ex vivo, without animal or human efficacy testing, and the activity of DH1017.IgM will be difficult to separate from its exceptionally potent neutralization in vivo. The work therefore identifies a measurable antibody function and a basis for further study, rather than demonstrating that an IgM treatment can prevent severe dengue, Zika disease or fetal harm. View five news elements
Time
The study was conducted and reported by October 20, 2026, with the publication date being October 20, 2026.
Location and population
The study involved laboratory assays using Zika virus and dengue virus serotypes, with participants infected with either the SJRP/2016-184 or Nicaragua/2016 Zika strain, and seven individuals receiving placebo.
Event
A recombinant IgM monoclonal antibody, DH1017.IgM, was isolated from a pregnant woman who had Zika infection and was found to reduce antibody-dependent enhancement (ADE) of Zika virus infection in laboratory assays, including at concentrations below those required for viral neutralization. The study also tested a second IgM antibody, C8.IgM, against dengue virus serotypes.
Scale, impact and risk
The study showed that DH1017.IgM reduced ADE by an average of 2.7-fold, with reductions ranging from 1.8- to 3.8-fold. It also demonstrated that the effect depends on the specific IgM–IgG pairing rather than being a uniform property of all IgM antibodies. The clinical importance of enhancement during Zika infection remains uncertain.
Response, status and uncertainty
The findings are based on laboratory assays and do not demonstrate that an IgM treatment can prevent severe dengue, Zika disease, or fetal harm.
